Title: Sinopsis, ili kratkoe opisanie ot razlichnykh letopistsev o nachale slavenskago naroda, o pervykh kievskikh kniazekh, i o zhitii sviatago blagovernago i velikago kniazia Vladimira ... i o ego naslednikakh, dazhe do ... tsaria Feodora Alekseevicha (the Kyiv Synopsis, St Petersburg printing of 1762)
Creator: Anonymous on the title page as read in the OCR; the host record attributes the work to Innokentii Gizel (1600-1683), archimandrite of the Kyiv Caves, which is the traditional attribution and is carried here as the host's, not as settled
Publisher: St Petersburg, 'at the Imperial' press (the rest of the imprint is garbled in the OCR); the printing carries a dedication to Empress Catherine
Publication Date: 1762 (host record date; the title-page OCR does not give a legible year)
Rights: Public Domain (printed in St Petersburg in 1762); the host record carries no possible-copyright-status field and the item is not access-restricted
Language: chu (Slaveno-Russian in civil type; the host catalogues it as Russian)
Source: Internet Archive item sinopsisilikratkoeopisanieotrazlichnyhle85, file sinopsisilikratkoeopisanieotrazlichnyhle85_djvu.txt (host collection russian-online-libraries), fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for the Kyiv-Mohyla Orthodox Revival), 2026-10-23; host rights statement: none given in the item record
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from this header. Nothing was corrected, re-spaced or retyped. Rights basis restated: a book printed in St Petersburg in 1762 is public domain by date; the year is the host's.
Content note: The Kyiv Synopsis in a St Petersburg printing; this file is the 1762 printing, not a Kyiv printing (the host holds Kyiv printings of 1680 and 1836 as separate items, 1680g and 5_20211201_20211201, not fetched). The printing carries a dedication to Empress Catherine and, near file line 9578, a printers' note 'Ko chitateliu sego letopistsa ot tipografov' (From the printers to the reader of this chronicle) whose press and date the OCR does not show; this file does not settle which printing the note comes from. The text is a short history of the Slavs and the Kyivan princes, continued on the title page to Tsar Feodor Alekseevich. OCR sampled at five points (10/30/50/70/90 percent): the Church Slavonic is readable with scattered errors; the title page and ornamented headings are garbled (the 10 and 90 percent samples). Second witness: check every quotation against the page images. No public-domain English translation was found.
